Turns out they weren't meant to be that hard to kill after all.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you replayed The Witcher 3 in its shiny new remastered form and got absolutely bodied by a couple of werewolf fights you thought were easier back when you had more youthful reflexes, don't worry—the problem wasn't your aging brain and hands after all. It was a bug that's been caught and exterminated in patch 5.02. "Fixed an issue where werewolves could regenerate health too quickly on higher difficulty settings," the patch notes say, "making them impossible to defeat."

Another problem some players had with The Witcher 3 Remastered was its lighting, with interiors in particular looking too dark while some other locations were washed-out flashbangs. Good news: "Fixed various lighting issues across multiple locations and cutscenes," the patch notes say, "including overly bright or dark areas, inconsistent lighting transitions and flickering." They've also "Fixed various lighting issues with hair when Path Tracing is enabled" and "Made various Path Tracing and Ray Reconstruction optimizations and visual improvements, including lighting, shadows, and water rendering."

That's all positive stuff, but the balance changes to various signs and mutations will be more controversial. The Remastered version launched with buffs to Witcher signs that made them useful even if you didn't specialize in them, but it may have overdone things a little. This patch walks back some of the potential for you to dominate the entire game with an Aard build, though changes like reducing the Catalyst skill's bonus to Aard and Igni intensity by 10% don't seem like they'll totally change the way you play.

I get that people don't like skill nerfs in singleplayer games, but I can also see CD Projekt anticipating next year's Songs of the Past DLC. They don't want to hear a bunch of players say, "It sucked because I trivialized the whole thing with a broken build I got by copying a YouTuber." If some skill nerfs and a reduction to stamina regeneration in combat mean you have to drink more potions to get your signs off, it probably won't ruin your fun.

Oh, and if you couldn't complete your Gwent collection because Zoltan Chivay's card didn't show up, the patch notes say it'll now appear in the correct place under the Hanged Man's Tree in Velen. Time to go back and get that 100% achievement at last.

Superman Versus Green Lantern Corps Finally Answers The Ultimate Superhero Match-Up

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Most of the time, Superman is in good standing with the Green Lanterns, particularly the ones hailing from Sector 2814. Most of the Justice League’s origin stories depict the Man of Steel co-founding the superhero team with Hal Jordan, the first human to join the Green Lantern Corps. John Stewart, Guy Gardner, Kyle Rayner, Simon Baz, Jessica Cruz, and Jo Mullein all later joined the Justice League, so Superman has frequently teamed up with them as well.

His interactions with the Green Lantern Corps as a whole, on the other hand, have largely been confined to crossovers, like when many of these emerald ring slingers came to Earth to battle the Black Lanterns in the Blackest Night event. However, in a few months time, the Green Lantern Corps will have a major presence in the ongoing Superman series. Unfortunately for Clark Kent, he’ll find himself at odds with the Green Lanterns, which will set up a big showdown.

More than 15 years after Blackest Night ended, Superman is once again having to contend with the Black Lanterns and their leader, Nekron. The “Night Shift” storyline kicked off in Superman #43 with the title protagonist being blasted with energy from a corrupted Black Lantern Ring, which in turn led to him being burned by sunlight. Further experimentation in Issue #44 showed Superman being burned and even igniting into flame when exposed to sunlight, which he can also no longer absorb.

The solicitation for Superman #47 reveals that “Night Shift” will conclude this issue with Clark having to “stop the Green Lantern Corps from taking the Black Lantern Ring out of Metropolis.” The reason for this apparently has something to do with a ticking clock threat, as his allies Marilyn Moonlight and Silver Banshee must venture into Metropolis’ hidden Dead Zone to “find the Cult Leader” who worships Nekron and “free his zombie followers before the Dead Zone is cut off forever.”

Superman #47 will also tie into the wider DC All-In saga by featuring an appearance from Pandora, who was a major player during the New 52 era, and is now a member of Darkseid’s sinister Justice League. As far as the Green Lanterns go, the issue’s cover shows that Green Lantern Corps leader Jessica Cruz did not skimp on bringing reinforcements to confront Superman.

Before Hal Jordan was selected to become the Green Lantern of Sector 2814, there was another classic DC character who nearly got the ring instead.

Even assuming that Clark will be back to normal by Superman #47, there’s still the question of how he’ll fare at full power against the Green Lantern Corps. This kind of battle has never happened within the mainstream DC Universe, and while Superman can easily overpower one or two Green Lanterns on his own, an army of them might be too much, especially since their emerald energy was once shown to be capable of replicating Kryptonite’s radiation.

Plus, Superman’s against killing, so he’d have to restrain himself to a certain degree fighting off many Green Lanterns. However, that wasn’t a concern for a different, more brutal version of Superman. In Injustice: Gods Among Us Year Two, Clark joined forces with the Sinestro Corps to wage war against the Green Lanterns. Not only did he easily defeat many of them at his usual power level, he pushed Mogo and the Guardian Gantry into the Sun when he was further empowered by a Yellow Lantern ring.

There’s also the possibility that Superman’s confrontation with the Green Lantern Corps a few months from now won’t get too out of hand. If Marilyn Moonlight and Silver Banshee can pull off their rescue mission in time, then a fight won’t be necessary, and Superman won’t have any problem turning over the Black Lantern ring to the Corps. Still, it’s likely that in an action-packed comic like this, there will at least be a brief skirmish, albeit with no major casualties like what happened in the Injustice universe.

Superman #47 will be released Wednesday, February 24, 2027, by DC Comics.

SpaceX shares jump on alternative trading platform after Musk says he will expand share donations

📅 2026-11-27  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Shares of SpaceX jumped 7.47% in overnight trading on Blue Ocean ATS after CEO Elon Musk expanded his SpaceX share-donation plan for U.S. children.

"I will be donating another share of SpaceX to all kids in America every year," Musk said in a post on X.

Blue Ocean ATS is an alternative trading system for U.S. equities that operates from 8 p.m. to 4 a.m. ET, outside regular U.S. stock market hours. According to the platform's holiday schedule, overnight trading resumed at 8 p.m. ET on Thanksgiving Day. U.S. stock exchanges were closed Thursday and are scheduled to close early Friday.

SpaceX shares closed 1.30% higher on Wednesday ahead of Thanksgiving.

The latest pledge follows SpaceX's announcement Wednesday that it would donate one share of its Class A stock to every active Trump Account, tax-advantaged investment accounts for American children, with Musk personally matching the gift.

In a separate post on X, Musk said SpaceX's contribution would be transferred next month, while his personal donation would follow when his lockup period expires in June next year.

Nearly 70 million Trump Accounts had been created as of October, according to the White House.

Since the program's launch in July, families and friends have contributed more than $1 billion to the accounts, bringing total deposits from all sources to more than $9 billion, CNBC reported earlier this week.

USDT leads borrowing on four of six DeFi protocols examined by Europe’s banking watchdog

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The EBA urges the EU to assess restrictions on crypto firms facilitating loans involving USDT and other unauthorized stablecoins.

Tether’s USDT led stablecoin borrowing on Aave, Fluid and Spark in a July 30 snapshot published by the European Banking Authority (EBA) on Nov. 25.

The EBA renewed its call for the European Commission to assess rules for firms that give customers access to decentralized lending, including possible restrictions on loans involving stablecoins that lack the required EU authorization.

In its comparison of six lending protocols, the regulator grouped USDT and its USDT0 bridged version when describing demand on Aave, Fluid and Spark. The EBA also found that borrowers on JustLend overwhelmingly favored USDT and pointed to the token’s dominance on the Tron blockchain.

USDC accounted for a larger share of active loans than USDT on Compound and Morpho.

The EBA measured each stablecoin against all active loans on the same protocol. Separate estimates suggest EU participation in decentralized finance remains limited, and self-hosted wallets and reporting gaps make it difficult to identify where users are based.

The EU’s Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA) requires authorization for covered stablecoins to be offered to the public or admitted to trading in the EU, and USDT lacks that authorization.

The EBA’s summary of MiCA’s scope says fully decentralized services operating without an intermediary fall outside the rules. The report says some EU consumers may hold stablecoins like USDT in wallets they control themselves.

Some crypto firms give customers access to lending protocols through their own interfaces, while others offer products tied to decentralized lending.

The EBA describes arrangements that let customers put their stablecoins to work without opening a self-hosted wallet. In those cases, customers depend on the platform that handles the investment.

Some firms connect customers to lending protocols through an interface. Others offer products tied to those markets.

A firm can serve as the entry point even when the lending protocol is decentralized.

The diagram shows access routes and a recommendation for assessment. Line weight does not show loan volume.

The flow diagram summarizes the access routes in section 3.3 of the EBA report and connects the firm route to the recommendation reiterated on Nov. 25.

The diagram shows access routes and a recommendation for assessment. Line weight does not show loan volume.

The EBA raised the issue in September as part of the Commission’s MiCA review, warning the Commission to weigh the costs and benefits of additional requirements for intermediaries. The options include checking whether products suit customers, requiring more disclosures, and limiting leverage.

The Commission could also assess restrictions on firms that facilitate borrowing and lending in stablecoins such as USDT, which require authorization but lack it.

The November report reiterates a recommendation for the MiCA review. The EBA plans to continue monitoring decentralized finance in 2027, including how consumers access stablecoins that lack MiCA authorization. That work could inform any further advice to the Commission.

8 Elite HBO Miniseries to Binge Right Now

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

HBO has long had a reputation for prestige, cinematic shows and miniseries. But the network has come a long way since The Far Pavilions and has become synonymous with top-tier, critically acclaimed miniseries. Several of the miniseries that HBO has produced over the last couple of decades are not just another feather in their hat but icons of their genre, TV classics of their time, and standouts among their contemporaries.

While 2001’s Band of Brothers has become the gold standard for war-drama television, 2019’s Chernobyl excels in translating a traumatic human event into an intricate character study. Between adaptations of bestselling novels and intriguing original stories, here are our picks for elite HBO miniseries that you can binge right now.

Scottish comedian and actor Richard Gadd of Baby Reindeer fame returns with another chilling miniseries, Half Man, which he also writes and stars in. The six-part psychological thriller unspools the complicated, violent, lifelong friendship between Gadd’s Ruben and Jamie Bell’s Niall, after an unexpected incident at Niall’s wedding forces them to reconnect with their past. Stuart Campbell and Mitchell Robertson portray the younger versions of Ruben and Niall.

Half Man is a story about toxic male friendships, but it is the show’s treatment that makes it more intense and raw than most. While Niall is an intricately written character, Ruben is a drastic departure from Gadd’s fragile, sensitive role in Baby Reindeer, and the actor-comedian thrives as the aggressive, tormented character. Deeply disturbing yet delicate in its own way, Half Man is an exceptionally intense show that easily ranks among the best TV dramas made in the 2020s.

Created by Steven Spielberg and Tom Hanks in their second collaboration following Saving Private Ryan, Band of Brothers is a 21st-century television classic adapted from the non-fiction book by Stephen E. Ambrose. Set during WWII, the war drama recounts the story of the “Easy” Company, 2nd Battalion, 506th Parachute Infantry Regiment of the 101st Airborne Division of the US Army on the Western Front between D-Day and their occupation of Berchtesgaden. Kirk Acevedo, Eion Bailey, Damian Lewis, Ron Livingston, and Neal McDonough portray various real-life characters.

Band of Brothers became an instant classic when it premiered 25 years ago and has since been an HBO landmark and often cited as a benchmark of the genre. The 10-episode miniseries combines a dramatized narrative with documentary-style, candid interviews with real veterans, reconstructing one of the grimmest times of human history in the most thoughtful way. A remarkable tribute to the sacrifices of a whole generation of American soldiers, Band of Brothers is surely a must-watch for genre fans.

Created by David Simon, Ed Burns, and Evan Wright, Generation Kill is an often-forgotten war miniseries that adapts Wright’s non-fiction book of the same name, chronicling his experiences as an embedded reporter in the US Marine Corps during the 2003 Invasion of Iraq. Set during the first phase of the war, the series follows Wright, played by Lee Tergesen, as he rides along with the U.S. Marine Corps' 1st Reconnaissance Battalion on their way to Baghdad. Other real-life people are portrayed by Alexander Skarsgård, Billy Rush, James Ransone, and Stark Sands.

Generation Kill is often considered one of the most visceral and grounded portrayals of war on television. At the time of its broadcast, it was among the most prestigious shows of the genre and is undeniably one of the best war shows of the 2000s. However, despite its critical acclaim and several accolades, Generation Kill has mostly faded from mainstream attention and has been overshadowed by bigger productions.

Britain makes more detective television than any country has any business making, and somehow almost all of it is good. Here is the one you belong in, what it says about you, and the case for it being the best of the four.

You want the tea, the gossip and the vicar behaving oddly. Midsomer is a county where the scenery is beautiful, the fete is on Saturday and the murder rate is roughly that of a small war, and absolutely nobody involved thinks this is strange.

You feel other people's grief as if it were handed to you personally. Broadchurch is less a whodunnit than a study of what a small town does to itself when it finds out that the answer was always going to be somebody everybody already knew.

You go through things rather than around them. John Luther is the best detective in London and a liability to everybody who works with him, and the show is completely honest that those two facts are the same fact.

You say the thing and you do not dress it up. Vera Stanhope turns up in a hat and a mac, is rude to almost everybody, and is the only person in the room prepared to ask the question that the family has been waiting years for somebody to ask.

Created by David E. Kelley, The Undoing is loosely based on Jean Hanff Korelitz’s novel You Should Have Known and follows Grace (Nicole Kidman), a wealthy therapist in New York City, whose perfect life begins to unravel when her renowned oncologist husband, Dr. Jonathan Fraser (Hugh Grant), is accused of murder. As the investigation proceeds, Grace and her family learn shocking truths about Jonathan, making her question her marriage, profession, and the truth of her life. Other key cast members include Noma Dumezweni, Édgar Ramírez, Donald Sutherland, and Noah Jupe.

Elevated by the star power of its cast and refined production design, The Undoing is truly an elite miniseries and one of the finest HBO has produced in the 2020s. Nicole Kidman and Hugh Grant steal every scene with their commanding presence, even if the plot loses momentum occasionally, making this six-episode psychological crime thriller a highly engaging watch. The show has earned praise for its acting, cinematography, and strong plot twists, but has also been criticized for its writing and pacing.

Now considered one of the most immersive screen portrayals of the disaster of 1986, Chernobyl is a dramatized retelling of the titular nuclear plant explosion in Ukraine that resulted in the devastation of all life in the region for decades. The series takes a closer look at the accident from the perspectives of all the key people involved, exploring the events leading up to it and its aftermath. The show boasts a strong ensemble cast, with Jared Harris, Stellan Skarsgård, Emily Watson, Paul Ritter, Jessie Buckley, and more in notable roles.

Created by Craig Mazin, Chernobyl is a masterfully made miniseries that cleverly unpacks the horrific history within its limited runtime. While the show takes some dramatic liberties, trading fact for fiction, it does so engagingly, with powerful performances and an atmospheric setting. The series has since remained a critical darling and a fan favorite, distinctly capturing the emotional and physical intensity of the real tragedy.

Starring Kate Winslet as protagonist Marianne “Mare” Sheehan, Mare of Easttown follows the titular police detective in a small Philadelphia town, where she attempts to solve the murder of a local teenager while being tormented by a similar case from a year ago. Mare continues to search for truth while grappling with past guilt, the loss of a child, divorce, and her declining reputation. Julianne Nicholson, Jean Smart, Angourie Rice, and Guy Pearce also star in key roles.

When Mare of Easttown first premiered, it was a class of its own as a crime drama thriller miniseries. Its gritty story, grounded characters, and deeply emotional plotlines all come together to make this an intense show that is sure to leave an impact on the audience long after the credits roll. Created by Brad Ingelsby, Mare of Easttown has become a modern classic for HBO that thrives on Kate Winslet’s exceptional, award-winning performance, which is often considered her TV best.

Based on Criminal Justice by Peter Moffat and created by Richard Price and Steven Zaillian, The Night Of centers on a Pakistani-American college student named Nasir “Naz” Khan (Riz Ahmed), who wakes up after a nightlong party fueled by drugs and alcohol next to the dead body of a woman he met the night before. With missing memories of the night and evidence pointing to him as the killer, Naz leaves his fate in the hands of a resolute lawyer named John Stone (John Turturro). Bill Camp, Michael Kenneth Williams, and Poorna Jagannathan appear in key roles.

The Night Of is an excellently made crime drama that grips the audience with its tightly-knit plot and deep, character-driven storytelling. The show’s creators elevate the original material by putting an intelligent spin on classic tropes and making it a timely story. When it premiered, the crime thriller was critically praised for its masterful production, layered characterizations, and riveting performances by Turturro and Ahmed.

A Southern Gothic psychological thriller miniseries created by Marti Noxon, Sharp Objects is a series adaptation of Gillian Flynn’s debut novel of the same name and follows Camille Preaker (Amy Adams), a reporter suffering from alcoholism and psychological troubles, who returns to her hometown in Missouri. Recently discharged from a psychiatric hospital and returning to her controlling mother, Adora (Patricia Clarkson), Camille is forced to confront her personal demons while investigating the murder of two young girls. Chris Messina and Eliza Scanlen also have main roles.

The HBO adaptation of Sharp Objects stays true to Flynn’s original crime novel in tone and themes but varies in format and pacing. In fact, the slow-burn storytelling balances the show’s atmospheric setting and dark themes to better explore the characters’ fractured psyches and meditate on their emotional states. Headlined by intense performances from Adams and Clarkson, Sharp Objects is one of HBO's best crime thriller miniseries of the 2010s.

Anya Taylor-Joy’s 2-Hour Thriller Is Still Her Greatest Movie 10 Years Later

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Anya Taylor-Joy’s film debut in Robert Eggers’ The Witch may have given the actor a cult following, but it was her role in M. Night Shyamalan’s Split in 2016 that made her a household name. A year after she shocked horror fans as Thomasin, she took on an incredibly different role in the thriller. A return to form for Shyamalan, Split takes on the complex issue of DID. Dissociative Identity Disorder is at the crux of the movie as James McAvoy takes on 24 separate identities in his role of Kevin.

Viewers only get to see some of them, but his immense talent in showing just a few is one of the best examples of character work. Even more impressive is Taylor-Joy, who stands toe to toe with the performer in her role as Casey Cooke. One day when Casey is out at a birthday party, she and acquaintances Marcia (Jessica Sula) and Claire (Haley Lu Richardson) are abducted by some of Kevin’s most insidious personalities. In the locked room drama, Casey weaponizes her wits and her own experience with trauma to escape her captivity and save her life.

M. Night Shyamalan features have typically been divisive in recent years, and Split is no different. In the movie, the director posits that DID can present itself not just in personality changes, but with physical characteristics as well. One of the most notorious of these personalities, the Beast, has more muscle definition and becomes literally bigger than the other personalities. This may be controversial with the mental health community, but there is no denying that this offers a fascinating new perspective on the subject of trauma.

Kevin materialized these personalities as a response to his overbearing and abusive mother. His foil in the film is Casey, played by Anya Taylor-Joy. Immediately, it is clear that Casey has a superpower that the other abductees do not. Slowly throughout the film, Casey’s own abuse and trauma are revealed, which allows her to empathize with Kevin. While Marcia and Claire want to break their way out by force, Casey understands that the best way to escape is through intelligence and subtlety.

This is a difficult balance to maintain, but Taylor-Joy does it almost effortlessly. She establishes this in the abduction scene as it slowly dawns on Casey that there is no escape. When Kevin’s personality, Dennis, enters the girls’ car, he subdues the other two easily in the backseat. Casey, who is in the front, immediately understands the danger in this encounter. As a single tear rolls down her face, she knows that there is no point in fighting and is taken as well.

What could be considered weakness or a fault in her character is exactly the opposite. Casey knows that she is a survivor. She has endured one monster before and will find a way to survive this one as well. Casey is characterized by her creativity in finding a way out of horrifying situations, which aids her until the very end of the movie. Just like in The Witch, Taylor-Joy accepts the task of a difficult character that could be misunderstood and instead makes it iconic. In the decade since Split first premiered, no other character has quite reached the heights of this one.

Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: Is Index Investing believing in efficient markets?

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Just wondering if investing in the index means you believe in efficient market theory (EMT)?
Since I am not likely to find and exploit inefficiencies in the market it's better for me to get the guaranteed return of the market rather than underperform. If you're at the poker table and you can't tell who the patsy is...it's likely you. You're trying to beat not only well paid money managers with advanced pedigrees but bots that can trade in nanoseconds. Some managers have rented space right next to the exchange to place their trade at microseconds faster than you. Bernstein said it's like trying to play tennis against the Williams sisters. The only winning strategy is not to play their game. Prices move around the "correct" price, i.e., they take a random walk. No one person can know everything, but collectively all participants can know everything. Accept the wisdom of the crowd. Index fund investors don't get average returns; they get the average return of the market, which, because most active fund managers/traders do worse than the market means you can get ABOVE AVERAGE returns by buying and holding the market.

Oracle Corporation: Assessing The Preferred Which Now Yields Over ~9%

China, Japan, South Korea and ASEAN to form working group on digital payments, crypto regulation

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The decision comes after the group met in May in Uzbekistan and identified cross-border digital payments as a new area of cooperation.

China, Japan, South Korea, and ASEAN member countries have agreed to set up a dedicated working group on cross-border digital payments and digital asset regulation.

According to a statement released Thursday, finance and central bank deputies gathered in Kyoto, Japan, this week to review progress in implementing the guidance set earlier by the ASEAN+3 finance ministers and central bank governors.

One major focus of the meeting was technological innovation, with discussions centering around cross-border payments and regulatory approaches to crypto assets. The deputies agreed to form the AMRO Cross-Border Digital Payments Program and a dedicated working group to support further policy talks.

The Kyoto decision is a result of a May meeting in Uzbekistan that identified cross-border digital payments as a new area of cooperation.

At that meeting, the group instructed deputies to establish modalities by the end of 2026 to deepen policy discussions on retail and wholesale payment connectivity, as well as regulatory approaches to stablecoins.

"Building on local currency‑based payment systems, technological innovation in cross‑border payments, such as tokenization, should be pursued in consistency with ongoing global discussions on policy […]," the group said in a May statement.

According to AMRO’s job post published last week seeking a consultant on cross-border retail payments, the initiative could cover research into a range of use cases, including person-to-person and person-to-merchant payments, tourism, e-commerce, and remittances.

Finance ministers and central bank governors are expected to further discuss the matter at the next meeting in Nagoya, Japan, in May 2027.

ASEAN+3 is an international cooperation framework that brings together 11 ASEAN member states and China, Japan, and South Korea. The 14 economies regularly convene to discuss political-security, economic, and socio-cultural matters.

The push for closer regional cooperation comes as Asian economies step up efforts to explore and test stablecoin and tokenization initiatives. Last month, the central banks of Malaysia and Thailand joined Singapore's BLOOM initiative to work on cross-border payments involving stablecoins and tokenized deposits.

Japan's top financial regulators are also studying tokenization of Japanese government bonds and a blockchain-based settlement system for stocks and Japanese government bonds. South Korea, meanwhile, is preparing tokenized securities from 2027 and local won stablecoins under its draft Digital Asset Basic Act.

Earlier this month, the central banks of the UK, Singapore, and Thailand also said they completed foreign exchange settlement trial linking traditional and tokenized systems.

38-Episode ‘Reacher Meets Justified’ Masterpiece Has Hands Down The Best Action On TV

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Prime Video’s Reacher appears to have set a new standard for pulsating, high-stakes action on the small screen, but it’s actually standing on the shoulder of another giant in the genre. Nine years before Alan Ritchson made his debut as Jack Reacher on streaming, a criminally underexposed Cinemax crime thriller was already wowing viewers lucky enough to have discovered it with jawdropping action sequences.

Centering on a mercenary outlaw who just happens to have found himself a sheriff’s badge in Pennsylvania, this show combines the heavy-hitting, close-quarters combat of Reacher with the swaggering, Western-inflected thrills of Justified. It takes pride of place among the best action TV series ever made, despite not having nearly the same profile of bigger names in the genre.

There are even those who’d go as far as claiming outright that the show is even better than Reacher at what it does. When it comes to sheer immersion in the art of painstakingly choreographed violence, this opinion stands to reason. What’s more, Banshee has a brilliantly original premise, dreamed up by Your Friends & Neighbors creator Jonathan Tropper and bestselling author David Schickler.

The means by which Lucas Hood assumes his identity as sheriff of the show’s titular Pennsylvania town is steeped in the traditions of neo-noir. What happens next grips us relentlessly across four seasons, punctuated by many of the greatest combat set-pieces ever made for television.

Anyone who enjoys the sight of Alan Ritchson working his way through a punishing action sequence in Reacher needs to find a way to watch Banshee on HBO and HBO Max, at the earliest possible opportunity. The Boys actor Antony Starr leaves Ritchson for dust playing an unnamed career criminal posing as lawman Lucas Hood.

We see Starr’s Banshee protagonist engage in all-out, full-throttle action often for several minutes per scene, while camera close-ups and near-invisible cuts in the edit give up little room to breathe. At the same time, the martial arts choreography by stuntman Marcus Young is some of the most authentic ever brought to the screen.

Before The Boys and Homelander, Antony Starr was in four seasons of a crime drama that deserved to be as big as ABC's and Nathan Fillion's The Rookie.

For the majority of each hour-long episode of Banshee, viewers are placed right in the thick of the action, which mixes steel and grit for the perfect action spectacular in a small-town setting. By the end credits, most viewers will find their heart racing at lightning pace. Bingewatch this series at your peril.

As a series that delivers non-stop action and keeps you on the edge of your seat throughout its run, Banshee is almost unrivaled in modern television. If a filmmaker managed to execute a car fight sequence as good as the one in the show’s third season, for example, they’d surely be in line for an Oscar nod. Yet, Banshee is still underappreciated by the television industry, and hasn’t been seen by nearly enough viewers.

If Banshee makes Reacher look tame to some audiences, then its Prime Video rival at least owes a debt of gratitude to the path it forged over on Cinemax during the mid-2010s. Like Jack Reacher, Lucas Hood is effectively a lone-wolf vigilante having to stave off all sorts of threats to the new life he’s made for himself in rural Pennsylvania.

However, whereas Reacher is ostensibly outside the law yet acts in the interests of good and adheres to a strict moral code, Hood is superficially a legitimate lawman but hides a much darker identity beneath his sheriff’s badge. His approach to running local law enforcement also owes something to maverick U.S. Marshal Raylan Givens from Justified.

Like Givens, Hood has to contend with the sorts of dangerous and unsavory characters who find their way through the cracks of rural areas in the Eastern United States. These characters include his nemesis, ex-Amish crime cartel boss Kai Proctor, played menacingly by Ulrich Thomsen.

Banshee is a compelling piece of storytelling, even leaving aside the visual mastery of its action sequences. It deserves not only the acclaim that Reacher and Justified have both received, but a similarly impressive popular following, too.

Management is betting that capacity investments and margin pressure today will unlock pricing power and profitability gains tomorrow.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 26, 2026 at 11:13PM EST

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Oct. 29, 2026, SPX Technologies (SPXC +1.06%) announced 21% year-over-year revenue growth driven by expanding data center demand. Surging order volume shows that the enterprise sits directly in the path of massive infrastructure spending.

Rapid growth also creates near-term friction, as management trades short-term margins for long-term production capacity. Three takeaways from the call reveal how management handles this rapid expansion.

Rapidly scaling production creates bottleneck risks that hurt efficiency. Management didn't hesitate to accept higher labor and outsourcing costs to keep pace with customer schedules rather than miss critical delivery dates.

Long-term expansion relies on durable enterprise demand. The heating, ventilation, and air conditioning (HVAC) segment saw organic backlog expand sharply as tech customers expanded AI-related infrastructure projects.

SPX Technologies expanded its addressable market by purchasing FIS Water, a specialized business providing water management solutions. Integrating these products with existing cooling towers deepens customer relationships across high-growth end markets.

Demand across power, industrial, and technology sectors remains robust, and management isn't hiding its enthusiasm for ongoing market expansion. The company holds strong proprietary intellectual property, flexible secondary sourcing, and established global distribution channels. Management focuses on production throughput and pricing discipline to offset temporary input cost inflation.

Capacity additions at key facilities will help internalize component fabrication as new assembly lines reach full operating efficiency. That transition positions the enterprise to improve margins while fulfilling multiyear commitments across power and cooling markets.

Ethereum’s Co-Founder Takes On Crypto’s Next Security Threat. Who Is Vitalik Buterin?

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Who is Vitalik Buterin, and why is Ethereum’s co-founder now questioning the security of the very devices crypto investors use to protect their wealth? The 32-year-old programmer has turned his attention to a problem that even open-source software cannot necessarily solve: compromised computer chips.

In an essay published on November 26, Buterin argued that cryptocurrency security needs to extend beyond software and into the physical hardware on which it runs. His message builds on one of crypto’s oldest principles, but with a new twist:

For someone who helped build an alternative to centralized financial infrastructure, questioning the manufacturers behind that infrastructure seems like a natural next step.

It also reflects a security philosophy that has become increasingly visible in Buterin’s work, from his push for greater privacy and quantum safety on Ethereum’s roadmap to his interest in independently verifiable computing.

Vitalik Buterin is a Russian-Canadian programmer, researcher and writer who conceived Ethereum in 2013, at the age of 19.

Born in Russia in 1994 and raised in Canada, Buterin discovered Bitcoin in 2011. He went on to co-found Bitcoin Magazine, where he explored the technology and economics behind cryptocurrencies long before digital assets attracted mainstream financial institutions.

His involvement in Bitcoin exposed him to a problem that would eventually lead to Ethereum. Blockchain technology had potential beyond transferring money, but building more sophisticated applications on Bitcoin presented technical limitations.

Rather than trying to accommodate every possible application within Bitcoin’s existing architecture, Buterin began working on a blockchain designed specifically for programmability.

At the time, he was studying computer science at the University of Waterloo, where he also worked as a research assistant to cryptographer Ian Goldberg.

According to the University of Waterloo, Buterin received a $100,000 Thiel Fellowship in 2014 and subsequently left university to work on Ethereum full-time.

In November 2013, Buterin wrote the initial draft of what would become the Ethereum whitepaper.

The proposal described a blockchain with a general-purpose programming environment. Developers would no longer need to create an entirely new network every time they wanted to build a decentralized application.

Consider a lending platform. Instead of relying on a bank to manage collateral, calculate interest and authorize withdrawals, developers could encode those rules into smart contracts running on Ethereum.

That does not eliminate every risk. Smart contracts can contain bugs, depend on unreliable external data or operate under centralized administrative controls. What Ethereum introduced was the ability to execute financial and other programmable agreements on a shared blockchain.

Although Buterin originated the concept, Ethereum was not built by him alone. Its founding team included seven other individuals: Gavin Wood, Joseph Lubin, Charles Hoskinson, Anthony Di Iorio, Mihai Alisie, Jeffrey Wilcke and Amir Chetrit.

The project’s first live network, known as Frontier, launched on July 30, 2015.

What began as an experimental computing platform eventually became the foundation for much of the decentralized finance industry. Stablecoins, decentralized exchanges, lending protocols and tokenized financial assets now use the infrastructure that Buterin and Ethereum’s early contributors helped establish.

The network’s own evolution, particularly its transition to proof-of-stake and the development of Layer 2 scaling networks, has shaped Ethereum’s first decade.

A hardware wallet is supposed to keep private keys away from potentially compromised computers and smartphones. But what happens if the device itself cannot be trusted?

Buterin’s latest research examines that possibility. He points to supply chain attacks involving tampered wallets and considers the potential for malicious modifications concealed inside apparently legitimate chips.

Open-source firmware allows researchers to examine the instructions a device runs. But those instructions reveal little about whether the manufactured chip has been altered.

Buterin wants manufacturers to disclose physical chip layouts, known as GDS files, so investigators have a reference against which to inspect actual hardware.

The distinction matters. Knowing what a chip is designed to do is not necessarily the same as knowing what the chip inside your wallet actually does.

One approach highlighted by Buterin comes from hardware security researcher Andrew Huang, better known as bunnie.

Huang has been developing a technique called Infra-Red, In Situ inspection (IRIS), which exploits the fact that silicon becomes transparent at certain infrared wavelengths.

Using infrared illumination and suitable imaging equipment, researchers can examine parts of a chip’s internal structure without necessarily removing it from its circuit board.

The technique could help detect substituted components or unexpected differences between manufactured chips and their intended designs.

In a 2024 update on his research, Huang explained that infrared imaging has resolution limits. It cannot reliably reveal every microscopic feature of modern chips. Combining imaging with additional electrical testing could improve confidence in a component’s integrity.

That is an important limitation for crypto investors. Publishing an open hardware design does not automatically make a wallet secure, just as publishing a smart contract’s code does not establish that it is free of vulnerabilities.

Buterin acknowledges that sophisticated hardware inspections are more likely to be performed by specialist organizations than ordinary wallet owners.

His essay is a proposal for improving security, not an announcement that existing hardware wallets have all been compromised.

Despite his status as Ethereum’s most recognizable co-founder, Buterin is not its CEO.

Ethereum is maintained through the participation of independent developers, researchers, node operators, validators and other ecosystem contributors. Changes to the network involve technical proposals, discussion, software development and adoption by participants.

Buterin has considerable influence over those discussions, but publishing a proposal does not give him the authority to implement it unilaterally.

His role within the Ethereum Foundation is also distinct from control over the blockchain itself. In its April 2025 governance announcement, the Foundation described Buterin’s responsibilities as providing technical and intellectual guidance to the wider ecosystem.

His research can influence the direction Ethereum takes. Whether the network ultimately follows that direction depends on considerably more than his endorsement.

Buterin accumulated considerable wealth through his involvement in Ethereum, with much of his publicly visible cryptocurrency wealth tied to ETH.

However, his exact net worth is not publicly established.

Blockchain analytics can estimate the value of assets held in wallets attributed to him, but those figures do not necessarily account for all his investments, liabilities, private assets or holdings at addresses that have not been publicly identified.

They also fluctuate with cryptocurrency prices. A wallet valuation should therefore not be confused with a complete assessment of his personal wealth.

Buterin’s latest essay is part of a longer-running interest in making digital infrastructure independently verifiable.

In September 2025, he published The Importance of Full-Stack Openness and Verifiability, exploring how transparency could extend across the hardware and software underpinning increasingly important parts of people’s lives.

His broader research includes cryptography, privacy, blockchain scalability and Ethereum’s transition toward greater quantum resistance. Buterin has previously called for greater experimentation across Ethereum’s application layer without compromising its core principles of censorship resistance, open-source development, privacy and security.

These interests share a practical concern: whether users can independently establish that the systems they rely on behave as intended.

There is a commercial obstacle, though. Semiconductor manufacturers invest heavily in proprietary processes, and designing chips specifically for inspectability can involve performance compromises.

But a device signing a cryptocurrency transaction does not necessarily need the computing power of a modern AI processor. That makes security-focused hardware a potentially useful starting point.

More than a decade ago, Buterin challenged the idea that blockchains should primarily be used to transfer money. His latest work challenges another assumption the industry has grown comfortable with: that trusting the code is enough.

For crypto investors, the next meaningful development may come from hardware manufacturers willing to make their products independently inspectable, rather than another software update promising greater security.

Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: Mathematical Error in 15% Savings Rule

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many people recommend a15% savings rate with no mathematical justification. Suppose you have stable real income, 30 years until retirement, and need income for 20 years after retirement. Under a 6% real certainty equivalent discount rate, the present value of your lifetime earnings is 13.8 years of current income. The present value of your retirement needs is 2 years of income, or 15% of the present value of your earnings. So a 15% savings rate superficially makes sense.

But it would be silly to live off 85% of your income so can retire and die spending 100%. Expenses typically go down to 70% in retirement. Social Security replaces 40% of income for most people. To replace the remaining 30% of income, you need only save 30%*2/13.8 = 4.4% of current income.

This assumes you have no current savings (include home equity) nor debt (including student loans). It also assumes a flat income profile. Many people have kids in middle-age and empty-nests with peak income in their 50's and 60's. Those people should spend more when their kids are young and then save later.

Moreover, you can invest more aggressively to earn a higher certain equivalent return when young. If the market has a bad decade then you can work and scrimp to boost savings. But you can never get back the time with your kids.
Have you read Bill Bernstein and Edward McQuarrie's paper? It offers some nice perspective on spending in retirement that differs from your mention of spending going down to 70% of pre-retirement income.

https://www.advisorperspectives.com/art ... -graveyard

The JP Morgan Annual Guide to Retirement uses some data to show spending patterns for those households who retire with $1-$3M in investable assets, as well as those households with $250K - $750K in investable assets. Both do show a dip in spending compared to pre-retirement household income. You can find those graphs with the data on pages 29 and 30 of the JP Morgan 2026 Guide:

https://am.jpmorgan.com/us/en/asset-man ... etirement/
Social Security replaces 40% of income for most people.
Who are most people? What is their claiming strategy?

This year's guide also shows the percentage of 40% coming from SS based on their household income. It's a household income of $70K. Again, we could debate whether or not $70K with a 40% income replacement coming from SS represents "most people".



If one begins saving early at a younger age, sure, the math shows that the annual percentage saved could be lower than if starting later. Pages 12-16+ of the JP Morgan Guide covers that as well.

The old standard used to be save 10% for retirement. Then it became 15% (still fairly standard these days). Some financial planners even preach the gospel of saving 20%. I've never run into anyone who said save 4.4%.

Keep in mind households are saving for many things. Retirement. College education for their children. A downpayment on a home. A car. A big special vacation. A remodel/upgrade for their home. As well as other things. It gets rather difficult pretty quickly to confine a household's saving to 4.4%, or 10%, or even 15% when you start factoring in all of the variables.

For those shooting for FIRE, or have a full on lifestyle retirement in their plans that they are saving for, the Optimize Your Retirement table is worth contemplating...

Personal Investments • Re: Trump Accounts question

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Can you roll a Trump Account over to your preferred brokerage?

Eventually, yes. Once the account is established with the Treasury, you should be able to roll it over to another brokerage using a trustee-to-trustee transfer of the entire account balance. The assets will retain their tax basis from the original account. However, the rules governing this process are still a work in progress.

Jerry Jones calls Mike McCarthy 'outstanding,' refutes Aaron Rodgers' accusations

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

ARLINGTON, Texas -- Aaron Rodgers nuked Pittsburgh Steelers coach Mike McCarthy's reputation after the Steelers benched him late Tuesday night.

In a scathing appearance on "The Pat McAfee Show" on ESPN on Wednesday, the four-time NFL MVP and Super Bowl XLV MVP ripped McCarthy, saying Steelers tight end Pat Freiermuth told him of Pittsburgh's decision to make him inactive for Friday's game against the Denver Broncos, and he accused McCarthy of sleeping and being late to team meetings throughout the season. Rodgers raised numerous other accusations against McCarthy, including that he arrived late this week and slept during offensive meetings.

Rodgers said that the behavior McCarthy exhibited this season in Pittsburgh was present during their shared tenure with the Green Bay Packers, as well as during McCarthy's run as head coach of the Dallas Cowboys. The Cowboys employed McCarthy as their head coach for five seasons from 2020 to 2024, and they went 49-35 in that span, tied for the eighth-best record in the league with the Minnesota Vikings. Dallas won 12 games in the regular season for three consecutive years from 2021 to 2023, the first time the franchise accomplished that feat since their 1990s Super Bowl dynasty teams. The Cowboys went 1-3 in the postseason under McCarthy, advancing no further than the NFC divisional round.

"They know what's going on. Like I said, they all know. Everybody knows," Rodgers said on Wednesday. "Just like the guys in Green Bay knew. It's like the people in Dallas knew. Like they know what the deal is."

One of the people in Dallas who claimed not to know what Rodgers was talking about is Cowboys owner and general manager Jerry Jones. Jones unequivocally refuted Rodgers' claims and said he tried to extend McCarthy when his original five-year contract expired at the end of the 2024 season. The two sides couldn't come to an agreement on a new deal, so McCarthy took a year off in 2025 before being hired by the Steelers this year.

"I never saw anything that would not ... I offered him to stay at least another year when he left. I thought he was outstanding as the head coach, and [he] had a great command of quarterback, offense. Can't say enough good things about him. He's frankly one of the finest people around football that I have been around. I can't say enough about him. I never had the type of thing that's being said about him, I've never had that in consideration at any of my time with him. [Whether it was] extending his contract or any of that. That [alleged behavior] was never on the table."

While Rodgers painted a picture of extremely erratic and irresponsible behavior by his longtime head coach, Jones claims none of that happened in Dallas.

'The Last of Us' Meets 'Silo' in Hulu’s 4-Part Sci-Fi Thriller

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Post-apocalyptic sci-fi thrillers have become some of TV's best shows. With examples like The Last of Us or Silo, these stories have grown incredibly popular for their spectacle and epic tales. The genre is more than gruesome stories about the world ending, as it offers a deep exploration of universal human struggles. While these recent examples have earned considerable attention, and rightfully so, they are not the first. In fact, Snowpiercer took a similar approach in 2020. The series creates an immersive world full of danger and division that all fans of the genre should watch.

Snowpiercer delivers a unique premise and incredible worldbuilding that sets it apart. Inspired by the 2013 film of the same name, the story isn't totally original, but the series builds on the concept that always deserved more than two hours. And Snowpiercer certainly expands the idea with four unforgettable seasons. Though it may not have gained the popularity that other comparable series have, it still deserves attention. Six years later, Snowpiercer is the perfect binge watch for anyone who loves sci-fi thrillers, dystopian worlds, or complex moral questions.

Snowpiercer features a world like no other, where a massive luxury train is the only place people can live. Eerily set in 2026, the story takes place seven years after an environmental disaster created a new ice age, killing much of human civilization. However, some survive on a constantly moving train designed by eccentric billionaire Mr. Joseph Wilford (Sean Bean). While the movement keeps them alive, the survivors continue to struggle. The system is strictly divided into distinct social classes. The elite live in the front, third-class laborers keep the machine running, and the stowaways, known as the Tailies, are just trying to survive. However, the dynamic alters when a series of murders threatens the train. Head Engineer Melanie Cavill (Jennifer Connelly) pulls Chicago police officer Andre Layton (Daveed Diggs) up from the Tail to uncover the truth.

Layton has his own motivations. He dreams of revolution and believes this investigation can take him one step closer, allowing him to map out the train. However, the fallout spirals as the train is full of secrets. Most prominently, the passengers begin to realize that inventor Joseph Wilford isn't running the train because Melanie abandoned him during boarding, leaving him for dead. Although this society is confined to a train, Snowpiercer covers a lot of ground as it explores the brutal realities of civilization, oppression, and unavoidable moral questions of survival. This is the crux of the series, giving it a dark and fascinating tone.

What stands out most about Snowpiercer is its worldbuilding. A train is an uncharacteristic setting, but it comes with plenty to explore as the series delves into how different biomes separate the classes, how food is grown, and even how the passengers deal with their dead. These may seem like small details, but they reveal a lot about the world and the desperate characters who inhabit it. Through these elements, the series brings this society to life and creates something new. As the story goes on, Snowpiercer even explores beyond the train, showing the devastation of the icy world and further immersing the audience in the dystopian setting.

Idris Elba has fans on the edges of their train seats.

Though this concept is rare, the story is inspired by the movie, but the two are far from the same. Of course, there are similarities, but Snowpiercer isn't even strictly a remake. The series takes the concept, but uses different characters and circumstances to tell its own story. With significantly more time, the series sets a slower pace, building tension and introducing the world a little at a time, which is a luxury the film doesn't have. Ultimately, that dedication to the story is what makes Snowpiercer work. The series stands out, even as the dystopian genre grows, and though it's a few years old now, Snowpiercer is an unmatched sci-fi watch that deserves more attention.

Snowpiercer is streaming on Hulu in the U.S.

Anya Taylor-Joy’s 93-Minute Crime Thriller 'Thoroughbreds' Is the Perfect Watch Before It’s Cut From Streaming

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

One of the most unsung releases of the last decade was 2017’s Thoroughbreds, a thriller deep in the film noir mode with three great actors going toe to toe. With Anya Taylor-Joy, Olivia Cooke, and Anton Yelchin as the architects and participants of a murder plot, it’s less a crime movie and more of a character study, with director Cory Finley mining both pathos and extremely dark comedy from the premise. In an era where minimal-location movies about the sociopathy of the rich were frequently successful discourse machines (including Taylor-Joy’s 2022 vehicle The Menu), Thoroughbreds was a classically amoral story of kids whose wealth insulates them from consequences, yet you still root for them. The movie was unfortunately ahead of the curve with its note-perfect plotting and criminal ideation, as well as its cast.

While the late Anton Yelchin had found major success in movies like Green Room or even the modern Star Trek films, Cooke and Taylor-Joy were just getting started, some years away from the blockbuster extravaganzas of Dune and House of the Dragon. Coming some years before that, Thoroughbreds suggested great depth from all three actors, playing them against each other as they all fight for their own agendas, while also giving viewers the chance to identify with potentially evil teens. The movie is leaving Starz on November 30, but its study of youthful sociopathy and the tenuous nature of female friendship means it should remain a cult classic for years to come.

Thoroughbreds is a movie of interiors, specializing in the rich, old money homes of its protagonists Amanda (Olivia Cooke) and Lily (Anya Taylor-Joy), who live in suburban Connecticut. From the very beginning, when Amanda mercy-kills her crippled horse (leading to the title) in an unnerving moment, the movie keeps its distance from the characters, with director Cory Finley’s camera holding on scenes and moments for long stretches. Rather than the aspirational gleeful immorality of the classic teenage murderer movie Heathers, Thoroughbreds is methodical and ironic. The fancy decor, the emotional repression, and the basement tanning beds all go hand in hand with Amanda’s claims of an inability to feel anything. You might wonder if she’s just being an edgy teenager, especially after the horse euthanizing moment. But the movie’s visual language backs her up.

In a twist directly out of Alfred Hitchcock’s Strangers on a Train, the movie’s narrative begins to hinge on a murderous favor. Lily, effectively tasked by Amanda’s mom to befriend her troubled daughter, has an abusive stepfather named Mark (Paul Sparks), and Amanda, in her emotionless wisdom, presents a solution: kill him. The girls have a means of laundering the murder in the form of drug dealer Tim (Yelchin). Olivia Cooke’s portrayal of Amanda veers beyond creating an empathetic version of somebody suffering from an undisclosed mental illness, but it never goes into villain territory either. The movie’s cold and heavily adorned visual sensibility might have Yorgos Lanthimos-esque ironic distance from what you see, but it means that the relationship between the two girls becomes increasingly complex, in a way that it wouldn’t be if viewers were directly keyed into how the girls see each other.

Cory Finley had reportedly envisioned the movie as a play originally, and that feeling lingers in the film. The minimal cast and locations, as well as the long takes and reliance on dialogue, have the potential to feel theatrical, but the movie is sophisticated on the level of cinematic storytelling, commenting on its cast with its staging and using its widescreen compositions for both comedy and terror as chickens come home to roost. As he told IndieWire, the story began with the idea of a character who couldn’t feel anything. As the friend seeking to take advantage of that to help her own family situation, Anya Taylor-Joy’s Lily is effectively not much different.

The movie’s cinematic lineage is rich, drawing from Alfred Hitchcock (besides Strangers on a Train, his highly theatrical one-take experiment Rope, with its homoerotic murder conspiracy, has a clear line to Thoroughbreds) as well as ‘90s evil rich kid movies like Cruel Intentions, but with a much less moral stance. Peter Jackson’s true-crime classic Heavenly Creatures also feels like an influence, with the fantastical sequences substituted for cold stylization. When Amanda and Lily’s plans go awry, we see the truth of what the girls are willing to go through with, how calculating they can be with the other’s emotional headspace. A different movie about teenage murder conspirators might let them off the hook, but Amanda’s dreams of wild thoroughbred horses suggest that a cruel end is coming to everybody.

Battlestar Galactica Franchise's Official 2027 Revival Leaves Fans Optimistic

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Battlestar Galactica is a sci-fi franchise that really deserves a lot more love than it gets in 2026, particularly from those in charge of making decisions around the commissioning of new TV shows and movies. The IP has enjoyed several iconic runs over the years, and a return with all of the visual bells and whistles that are possible these days would surely draw in a big audience.

In lieu of that, fans of Battlestar do have other mediums to turn to if they want to have a fun experience within the confines of that universe. Battlestar Galactica: Scattered Hopes is a survival fleet-management roguelite that released earlier this year, and its proving to be a genuine hit with both critics and players, particularly for how it realizes the setting we all know and love.

It turns out there's more on the way, though, thanks to a new board game that's set to be one of the most ambitious releases in the genre next year.

As first reported by W. Eric Martin's Board Game Beat, Ghost Galaxy Games has officially confirmed they are set to release a brand-new version of Battlestar Galactica: The Board Game in 2027, building on the solid foundations of the 2008 original. If any of you had a copy back then, you'll know it really was a solid game, and one that is ripe for new ideas and mechanics from seasoned designers.

If you aren't familiar with it at all, the original description for the game reads as follows:

"Battlestar Galactica: The Board Game is a game of mistrust, intrigue, and the struggle for survival. Based on the Sci Fi Channel series, Battlestar Galactica: The Board Game puts players in the role of one of ten of their favorite characters from the show. Each playable character has their own abilities and weaknesses, and must all work together in order for humanity to have any hope of survival. However, one or more players in every game secretly side with the Cylons. Players must attempt to expose the traitor while fuel shortages, food contaminations, and political unrest threatens to tear the fleet apart."

Representatives from the company were also keen to share their excitement at the news, including Christian T. Petersen, CEO of Ghost Galaxy, who said, "I had the pleasure to oversee the publication of this phenomenal board game during its first lease on life. We're having a lot of fun returning to the great characters and story of Battlestar Galactica, and we're working hard to ensure that our new edition will make a great game design even better!"

"We're thrilled to have Ghost Galaxy take on this classic game", said Bill Altig, SVP of Narrative Games at Fantasy Flight Games. "We can't wait to see it back in print."

The latest edition of Battlestar Galactica: The Board Game doesn't have an official release date beyond next year at the time of writing, though it's more likely to arrive in the second half of 2027 if usual gaps between initial announcement and launch hold firm.

Steve Martin And John Candy's Classic Thanksgiving Movie Is A Paramount+ Streaming Hit

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

John Hughes made some of the most beloved movies of all time in his eight directorial efforts, to the point where it's nearly impossible to rank the films he wrote and directed. Still, there are few movies that reach the specific balance of comedy with such an emotional punch as Hughes does in 1987's "Planes, Trains and Automobiles." This is a goofball movie that borders on the surreal, with some of the most bizarre imagery in a comedy movie, while also delivering some of the most heartbreaking and heartwarming moments that came out of the 1980s.

The film stars Steve Martin as Neal Page, an uptight marketing executive forced to be paired with Del Griffith (John Candy), a goofball salesman with a heart of gold, when their journey from New York City to Chicago is disrupted. What was meant to be just a short cab and plane ride home just in time for Thanksgiving ends up being a three-day odyssey full of shenanigans, attempted murder, and much more.

It's no wonder, then, that "Planes, Trains and Automobiles" is the No. 1 movie streaming on Paramount+ on Thanksgiving Day 2026. After all, there are plenty of movies about Christmas, to the point where it's easy to overlook Thanksgiving, and with good reason. For one, it's not really a holiday outside the U.S. and Canada (and even then, celebrated on different days), and the imagery is not dissimilar to Christmas anyway. Still, the best Thanksgiving movies deliver a very specific tone that's less about holiday magic and more about celebrating what we have despite the horrors of daily life. From the opening minutes of "Planes, Trains and Automobiles," it's clear this movie could not be set at any other time of year except during the chaos and anxiety of Thanksgiving.

The travel chaos that is quite unique to Thanksgiving is the crux of "Planes, Trains and Automobiles." The arrival of snowstorms that plague the East Coast of the continental United States, the huge flux of travelers, and the general anxiety and irritability of travelers around the country add to, well, a very stressful time of year. It's why Steve Martin's uptight businessman Neal is so relatable. The film simply nails the feeling of needing to interact with others and ask for their help while being utterly frustrated with having to deal with anyone at such a bad time. It's also why John Candy's performance is such a tightrope, because his character has to be annoying in addition to being charming. Del's not really someone you'd want as a travel companion, yet he is absolutely the best person you want as a friend.

That tricky balance is what makes the movie so memorable, and why John Candy's best line remains the best Thanksgiving movie quote ever. Neal and Del are forced to not only set aside their differences, but actually recognize the humanity in each other while acknowledging that they themselves are flawed. This is a highly empathetic film that's all about how we should find love and camaraderie even in unlikely companions. That is why the film so perfectly captures the spirit of Thanksgiving, because it's not about estranged family members, but rather complete strangers who must come to learn they need one another and that they should be grateful for what they bring into each other's lives. Togetherness is not just for family members, or even people you like, but also for strangers you come to appreciate despite the ways they annoy you.

It’s a Shaolin Thanksgiving! Wu-Tang Clan, Chad Smith Light Up Halftime at Detroit Lions NFL Game

📅 Nov 27, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The hip-hop legends hit a nearly seven-minute tag-team medley of greatest hits that was announced as "the wrap-up" of its Wu-Tang Forever: The Final Chamber farewell tour.

Wu-Tang Clan created a Shaolin Thanksgiving experience in Detroit on Thursday afternoon (Nov. 26), joined by Red Hot Chili Peppers drummer — and homeboy — Chad Smith for a halftime performance at Ford Field for the Lions’ annual Thanksgiving game.

The collective — recently inducted into the Rock And Roll Hall of Fame — lit up the stadium with a nearly seven-minute tag-team medley of greatest hits that was announced as “the wrap-up” of its Wu-Tang Forever: The Final Chamber farewell tour.

Flanked by flags bearing the Wu-Tang and Lions logos, and with 500 fan volunteers moshing on the field in front of the stage and on its wings, the nine surviving MCs — plus Young Dirty Bastard in place of his late father Ol’ Dirty Bastard and DJ Mathematics — kicked off with “Triumph,” then rolled through bits of “4th Chamber,” “Da Mystery of Chessboxin’,” “Protect Ya Neck,” “Method Man,” “Shimmy Shimmy Ya,” “Ice Cream” and “C.R.E.A.M.” The RZA-led “Wu-Tang Clan Ain’t Nuthing ta F’ Wit” finished the set with the group members on the field amidst the fans, with smoke jets shooting up from the stage.

The Lions cheerleading squad also took part in the show, sporting T-shirts with a glittering Wu-Tang/Lions mash-up logo.

“Who’s got it better than me?” Smith — who participated in a 2025 remix of “Wu-Tang Clan Ain’t Nuthing Ta F’ Wit” — enthused to Billboard during rehearsals at the stadium on Wednesday. “This is the…Wu-Tang Clan. It’s the halftime show, the biggest game of the year, and the Lions. I’m in my home town, and I get to play drums at halftime with the legendary Wu-Tang — come on! I’m the queen of the ball. And I love those guys. I mean, as far as iconic ’90s hip-hop goes, there’s Nas, maybe, but I love Wu-Tang.”

The Wu-Tang booking was also a personal pleasure for Lions general manager Brad Holmes, an unabashed fanboy who attended the rehearsal as well as the game.

“I would put myself in the die-hard category,” said Holmes, who became a Wu-Tang fan while growing up in Tampa, Fla., and even met U-God after “stalking” the troupe at its hotel before a concert there. “Their music, it really just hit my soul. It’s truly art; I didn’t know how much art it really was, and as I’ve gotten older, the more I listen to it, it just gets even better. When they said Wu-Tang Clan on Thanksgiving, I just” (makes gesture of head exploding).

The Detroit Thanksgiving show — one of three on Thursday, plus U2’s performance on Wednesday night in Los Angeles — was the second of a three-year deal between the Lions with Eminem and his manager and Shady Records President Paul Rosenberg. (Jesse Collins Entertainment stages the show.) Last year’s inaugural installment set a high mark, with Eminem joining fellow home town hero Jack White on stage before a TV audience of nearly 48 million, and Rosenberg said they knew there was a lot to live up to when they began planning this year’s halftime show during spring.

“Last year, with the exception of (Eminem) participating and rapping, it was a rock ‘n’ roll show, so we decided that we should probably move towards our roots this year and do something more hip-hop based,” Rosenberg explained. “So we put a short list together, and Wu-Tang was at the top of our list.”

The fact that, last year, White had just been inducted into the Rock Hall and Wu-Tang Clan is a new inductee this year was “coincidental,” according to Rosenberg. But it did factor into this year’s determination.

“I don’t think it hurts,” he said, “because it’s the right timing, and a good thing for them for their profile. We knew they were going to be inducted, so the timing worked out well.” And like Holmes, Rosenberg added, Wu-Tang has personal importance for both he and Eminem.

“Back in the days when I was in law school in Detroit, going to the Hip Hop Shop every Saturday, the Wu-Tang Clan was a bit part of the soundtrack and fabric of what everybody was into and the scene was gravitated towards,” Rosenberg recalled. “So our fandom is certainly something we are very tapped into, and we don’t shy away from that when making these decisions.”

Adding the Chili Peppers’ Smith to the performance, meanwhile, was an intentional nod to their shared home town. “We always want to include something tied to the city if we can,” Rosenberg said, “and Chad’s a big Lions fan and a Michigander, and he was super excited to jump on board.” Smith, who’s participated in Lions festivities in the past, added that he felt a particularly strong energy around the game, an NFC North rival Chicago Bears, with the teams tied for the division lead with 8-2 records.

“I can just feel it, y’know?” he said. “Everybody’s got the spirit. When a Detroit sports team is doing well, the city embraces it. We love our sports, we love our music. It affects the whole community, the vibe and the mood and everything. It feels really good.”

Actor Tim Allen, a regular attendee at the Thanksgiving game, has his own fresh connection to Wu-Tang during the second season of his ABC show Shifting Gears. “We just finished taping, and Chill (Daryl Mitchell) and I dance to a Wu-Tang Clan song at the end,” Allen said on the sidelines before Thursday’s game. “I think he’s friends with them…so now all of a sudden I’m a big fan of theirs.”

Rosenberg said he and Eminem resisted the idea of having the rapper make another guest appearance, despite his regard for and friendship with Wu-Tang Clan. “The idea was not to have him perform every year at all,” he said. “That was not something we had discussed, and we’re not something we’re obligated to do.” Rosenberg added that he, Eminem and the Lions have not yet talked about extending the Thanksgiving halftime show production deal beyond 2027. “We’re certainly having a good time,” he said, “and we really enjoy the process. I think that people are digging it, so we’ll see.”

During the game the Lions also honored the late Marth Firestone Ford — the team’s principal owner from 2014-2020 (her husband William Clay Ford Sr. bought the team in 1963) with a moment of silence. Ford passed away on Tuesday at the age of 101.

In addition to Thursday’s performance, Wu-Tang Clan and the Lions, through the apparel company Detroit Vs. Everybody, partnered on a line of T-shirts and hoodies, the latter of which were worn by  fans on the field during the halftime show. The line goes on wide sale at 10:30 a.m. ET Friday via shop.detroitlions.com.

Wu-Tang’s Rock Hall induction will air on Dec. 8 on ABC, streaming on Disney+ the following day. Smith and the Red Hot Chili Peppers performed at the ceremony as well, honoring Rick Rubin’s Musical Excellence award dressed like the producer.
